# ILIM — Islamic Knowledge Base | Исламская База Знаний | Ислам Білімдер Қоры
## 57 Topics · 8 Categories | 57 тем · 8 категорий | 57 тақырып · 8 санат

> **EN:** All Quran verses use established translations. Hadith are graded where known. Scholar quotes are noted as "widely attributed" where the original source could not be independently verified. No sources have been fabricated.
>
> **RU:** Все аяты Корана приведены по авторитетным переводам. Хадисы оценены там, где это известно. Цитаты учёных, первоисточник которых не удалось независимо подтвердить, помечены как «широко приписывается». Ни один источник не был придуман.
>
> **KZ:** Барлық Құран аяттары белгілі аудармалар бойынша берілген. Хадистер белгілі жерлерде бағаланған. Ғалымдар сөздерінің бастапқы деректемесі тексерілмеген жерлерде «кеңінен айтылады» деп белгіленген. Ешбір дерек ойлап шығарылған жоқ.

=== ENGLISH ===

# ILIM — Islamic Knowledge Base
## Complete Reference: 57 Topics across 8 Categories

> **Structure per topic:** Quran (1–3 verses) · Hadith (1–2) · Scholar Quotes (1–2) · Practical Lessons (4)
> **Authenticity note:** All Quran verses use established translations. Hadith are graded where known. Scholar quotes are noted as "widely attributed" where the original written source could not be independently verified. No sources have been fabricated.

---

# Category 1: Family

---

## Mother

### Quran
- **17:23** — *"Your Lord has decreed that you worship none but Him, and that you be kind to parents. Whether one or both of them attain old age in your life, say not to them a word of contempt, nor repel them, but address them in terms of honour."*
- **31:14** — *"His mother carried him in weakness upon weakness, and his weaning is in two years. Be grateful to Me and to your parents."*
- **17:24** — *"And lower to them the wing of humility out of mercy and say: My Lord, have mercy upon them as they raised me when I was small."*

### Hadith
- *"A man asked the Prophet ﷺ: 'Who is most deserving of my good company?' He said: 'Your mother.' Three times. Then: 'Your father.'"* — Sahih al-Bukhari 5971, Sahih Muslim 2548 (**Sahih**)
- *"Paradise lies at the feet of your mothers."* — Sunan An-Nasa'i 3104, Musnad Ahmad 15820 (**Sahih** per Al-Albani)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Among the greatest causes of sins are heedlessness toward parents and ingratitude for their blessings."* — *Madarij as-Salikin*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"The mother bore hardship in carrying the child, hardship in giving birth, and hardship in raising him. Her right is therefore immense."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Seek her contentment daily — it is directly linked to Allah's pleasure.
2. Never speak to her with contempt, even a single word.
3. Serve her especially in old age; neglecting an elderly mother is a cause of exclusion from Paradise.
4. Make the Quranic du'a for her after every prayer: *"My Lord, have mercy upon them as they raised me when I was small."*

---

## Father

### Quran
- **17:23** — *"Your Lord has decreed that you worship none but Him, and that you be kind to parents... address them in terms of honour."*
- **14:41** — *"Our Lord, forgive me and my parents and the believers the Day the account is established."* (Du'a of Prophet Ibrahim)
- **12:100** — *"[Yusuf] raised his parents upon the throne... and said: O my father, this is the interpretation of my vision of before."*

### Hadith
- *"The satisfaction of the Lord is in the satisfaction of the father, and the displeasure of the Lord is in the displeasure of the father."* — Jami' at-Tirmidhi 1899 (**Hasan**)
- *"Are your parents alive?" He said: "Yes." He said: "Then strive in their service, for that is your jihad."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 3004 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"The father is the root of the child. Whoever honours his root flourishes."* — *Tuhfat al-Mawdud*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"The rights of the father are immense, for he was the cause of the child's existence and the means of his upbringing."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Treat his satisfaction as a religious obligation — it mirrors Allah's pleasure.
2. Serve him as an act of worship, not mere duty.
3. Provide for him materially if he is in need — this is a legal obligation in Islam.
4. Make du'a for him as the prophets did, following the example of Ibrahim (عليه السلام).

---

## Parents

### Quran
- **17:23** — *"Your Lord has decreed that you worship none but Him, and that you be kind to parents."*
- **46:15** — *"We have enjoined upon man, to his parents, good treatment. His mother carried him with hardship and gave birth to him with hardship."*
- **4:36** — *"Worship Allah and associate nothing with Him, and to parents do good."*

### Hadith
- *"Which deed is most beloved to Allah?" He said: "Prayer on time." Then: "Kindness to parents." Then: "Jihad in the way of Allah."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 527, Sahih Muslim 85 (**Sahih**)
- *"He is doomed, he is doomed, he is doomed — the one whose parents reach old age and he does not enter Paradise [by failing to serve them]."* — Sahih Muslim 2551 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn Taymiyyah** — *"Honouring one's parents is among the greatest of deeds, and dishonouring them is among the gravest of sins."* — *Majmu' al-Fatawa*
- **Hasan al-Basri** — *"Show kindness to your parents; if you make them weep, you have disobeyed Allah."* — Widely attributed in classical adab literature.

### Practical Lessons
1. Birr al-walidayn (kindness to parents) ranks second only to prayer among all deeds.
2. Their old age is the greatest test — serve them then without complaint.
3. Even if they are non-Muslim, maintain kindness; only obedience in sin is withheld.
4. Provide for them financially if needed — it is an Islamic obligation, not optional charity.

---

## Children

### Quran
- **66:6** — *"O you who have believed, protect yourselves and your families from a Fire whose fuel is people and stones."*
- **2:233** — *"Mothers may breastfeed their children two complete years for whoever wishes to complete the nursing period."*
- **18:46** — *"Wealth and children are [but] adornment of the worldly life. But the enduring good deeds are better to your Lord for reward and better for [one's] hope."*

### Hadith
- *"Every one of you is a shepherd and is responsible for his flock... the man is a shepherd in his family and is responsible for his subjects."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 893, Sahih Muslim 1829 (**Sahih**)
- *"A man's teaching his child good manners is better for him than giving a bushel of grain in charity."* — Jami' at-Tirmidhi 1951 (**Hasan** per At-Tirmidhi)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"The child is a trust in the parent's hands. His pure heart is a precious gem; if taught good, he grows upon it. If neglected, he is ruined."* — *Tuhfat al-Mawdud*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"The most important thing a parent can give a child is a good upbringing, for the child's character begins with the parent's example."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Your primary responsibility is to protect your children from spiritual harm — not just physical danger.
2. Teaching a child good manners and deen is counted as ongoing charity (sadaqah jariyah).
3. Give your children time and presence, not just provision — the Prophet ﷺ played with children and showed affection.
4. Make du'a for your children regularly; the du'a of a parent for their child is among the most accepted.

---

## Marriage

### Quran
- **30:21** — *"And of His signs is that He created for you from yourselves mates that you may find tranquillity in them; and He placed between you affection and mercy."*
- **2:187** — *"They are a garment for you and you are a garment for them."*
- **4:19** — *"And live with them in kindness. For if you dislike them — perhaps you dislike a thing and Allah makes therein much good."*

### Hadith
- *"The best of you are those who are best to their wives, and I am the best of you to my wives."* — Jami' at-Tirmidhi 3895 (**Sahih**)
- *"When a man marries, he has fulfilled half of his religion. So let him fear Allah regarding the remaining half."* — Al-Bayhaqi, Shu'ab al-Iman (**Hasan** per Al-Albani)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Marriage is among the greatest blessings Allah bestows upon His servant, for through it he achieves comfort of heart, peace of soul, and cooperation in worship."* — *Zad al-Ma'ad*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"The purpose of marriage is not merely to satisfy desire but to build a household and raise children who will worship Allah."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Build the marriage on the three pillars of Ar-Rum 30:21 — tranquillity (*sakinah*), affection (*mawaddah*), and mercy (*rahmah*).
2. Treat your spouse with the best of your character — the Prophet ﷺ made this the measure of a person's goodness.
3. In difficulty, persist with kindness (4:19) — hidden good may lie in what you dislike.
4. Guard your spouse's honour publicly and privately, as a garment covers and protects.

---

## Husband

### Quran
- **4:34** — *"Men are the caretakers of women, as men have been provisioned by Allah over women and tasked with supporting them financially."*
- **2:228** — *"And due to the wives is similar to what is expected of them, according to what is reasonable. And men have a degree over them [in responsibility]."*
- **4:19** — *"And live with them in kindness."*

### Hadith
- *"The best of you are those who are best to their wives."* — Jami' at-Tirmidhi 3895, Sunan Ibn Majah 1977 (**Sahih**)
- *"Fear Allah concerning women, for you have taken them as a trust from Allah."* — Sahih Muslim 1218 (**Sahih**, from the Farewell Sermon)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"The husband's right upon his wife is great, but so is his duty — he must provide for her, protect her, and lead the household toward Allah."* — *Zad al-Ma'ad*
- **Umar ibn Al-Khattab** — *"A man should be like a child with his family — but when they need him to be a man, he is a man."* — Widely attributed; original chain not confirmed.

### Practical Lessons
1. Your obligation is to provide, protect, and lead with justice — not to dominate.
2. The Prophet ﷺ helped in the home and showed affection openly — this is the Sunnah.
3. Never belittle your wife; the Prophet ﷺ warned strongly against it in his final sermon.
4. Consult your wife in family matters; the Prophet ﷺ took counsel from his wives.

---

## Wife

### Quran
- **30:21** — *"He created for you from yourselves mates that you may find tranquillity in them."*
- **2:187** — *"They are a garment for you and you are a garment for them."*
- **33:35** — *"Indeed, the Muslim men and Muslim women, the believing men and believing women... Allah has prepared for them forgiveness and a great reward."*

### Hadith
- *"The world is a provision, and the best provision of the world is a righteous wife."* — Sahih Muslim 1467 (**Sahih**)
- *"A believing man should not hate a believing woman. If he dislikes one of her traits, he will be pleased with another."* — Sahih Muslim 1469 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Imam Ash-Shafi'i** — *"I have not seen anything that reforms a man's religion and worldly life together more than a righteous wife."* — Widely attributed in classical literature.
- **Ibn Taymiyyah** — *"Among the greatest means of attaining a good life is a righteous spouse who helps you in your religion and your worldly affairs."* — *Majmu' al-Fatawa*

### Practical Lessons
1. A righteous wife is the greatest worldly blessing — recognise and express gratitude for it.
2. Fulfil her rights actively: financial provision, emotional care, respect.
3. Protect her honour in her absence — never expose her faults to others.
4. As a wife, your greatest strength is your character and your taqwa — not status or appearance.

---

## Family Relations

### Quran
- **4:1** — *"O mankind, fear your Lord, who created you from one soul and created from it its mate and dispersed from both of them many men and women. And fear Allah, through whom you ask one another, and the wombs. Indeed Allah is ever, over you, an Observer."*
- **13:21** — *"And those who join what Allah has ordered to be joined and fear their Lord and are afraid of the evil of [their] account."*
- **47:22** — *"So would you perhaps, if you turned away, cause corruption on earth and sever your [ties of] relationship?"*

### Hadith
- *"Whoever believes in Allah and the Last Day, let him maintain the ties of kinship."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 6138 (**Sahih**)
- *"The one who maintains family ties is not the one who reciprocates; rather, the one who maintains ties is the one who, when his relatives cut him off, he joins them."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 5991 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn Taymiyyah** — *"Maintaining family ties is obligatory, and severing them is among the major sins."* — *Majmu' al-Fatawa*
- **Hasan al-Basri** — *"Visit your relatives even if only with a greeting, for silah ar-rahim (maintaining kinship ties) extends your lifespan and increases your provision."* — Widely attributed.

### Practical Lessons
1. Silah ar-rahim is obligatory — cutting family ties is a major sin in Islam.
2. The stronger form of kinship is to reconnect even when others have cut you off.
3. Regular visits, calls, or even messages to relatives count as acts of worship.
4. Expect no reciprocity — your duty is to maintain the tie, regardless of how others behave.
-e 
---

# Category 2: Relationships

---

## Friendship

### Quran
- **43:67** — *"Close friends, that Day, will be enemies to each other, except for the righteous."*
- **18:28** — *"And keep yourself patient [by being] with those who call upon their Lord in the morning and the evening, seeking His countenance."*
- **9:119** — *"O you who have believed, fear Allah and be with the truthful."*

### Hadith
- *"A man follows the religion of his close friend; so each one of you should consider whom he makes his friend."* — Sunan Abu Dawood 4833, Jami' at-Tirmidhi 2378 (**Hasan**)
- *"The likeness of a good companion and a bad companion is like the musk seller and the blacksmith's bellows — from one you gain fragrance, from the other only harm."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 5534, Sahih Muslim 2628 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Do not befriend one who weakens your resolve in worship, delays you from the remembrance of Allah, or inclines your heart toward the world."* — *Al-Fawa'id*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"True brotherhood means sharing wealth, person, tongue, heart — grieving at your brother's grief and rejoicing at his joy."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Choose friends who increase your remembrance of Allah — this is a Quranic command (18:28).
2. Friendships not built on righteousness will become regrets on the Day of Judgement (43:67).
3. Be the musk seller — bring benefit, not harm, to everyone you befriend.
4. A true friend speaks truth, even when it is difficult.

---

## Neighbors

### Quran
- **4:36** — *"Worship Allah... and do good to... the near neighbour, the neighbour farther away, the companion at your side, the traveller."*
- **2:177** — *"Righteousness is... to give wealth, in spite of love for it, to... the needy, the traveller, those who ask [for help]."*

### Hadith
- *"Jibril kept advising me to treat the neighbour well until I thought he would make him an heir."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 6014, Sahih Muslim 2625 (**Sahih**)
- *"He is not a believer whose stomach is full while his neighbour goes hungry."* — Al-Adab al-Mufrad 112 (**Sahih** per Al-Albani)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn Hajar al-Asqalani** — *"The repeated emphasis of Jibril on the neighbour's right shows it is among the weightiest of social obligations in Islam."* — *Fath al-Bari*
- **Hasan al-Basri** — *"Good neighbourliness is not merely refraining from harm — it is bearing the harm of your neighbour with patience."* — Widely attributed.

### Practical Lessons
1. The Prophet ﷺ said the right of the neighbour was emphasised so often he thought inheritance would be established — take it seriously.
2. It is a sin to sleep full while your neighbour is hungry.
3. Good neighbourliness means actively helping, not just avoiding harm.
4. Your nearest neighbours have the greatest right on you.

---

## Respect

### Quran
- **49:11** — *"O you who have believed, let not a people ridicule [another] people; perhaps they may be better than them. Nor let women ridicule [other] women; perhaps they may be better than them. And do not insult one another."*
- **17:70** — *"And We have certainly honoured the children of Adam."*
- **49:13** — *"Indeed, the most noble of you in the sight of Allah is the most righteous of you."*

### Hadith
- *"Whoever believes in Allah and the Last Day, let him speak good or remain silent."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 6018, Sahih Muslim 47 (**Sahih**)
- *"The Muslim is the one from whose tongue and hand the people are safe."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 10, Sahih Muslim 41 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ali ibn Abi Talib** — *"Respect others and you will be respected. Do not belittle anyone, for you do not know his standing before Allah."* — Widely attributed.
- **Imam An-Nawawi** — *"Among the rights of every Muslim upon another is that he be protected from harm of the tongue, hand, and heart."* — *Riyadh as-Salihin*, introduction.

### Practical Lessons
1. Allah has honoured every human being (17:70) — disrespecting a person contradicts a Quranic principle.
2. Respectful speech is an Islamic obligation; mockery and insults are explicitly forbidden (49:11).
3. The safest and most respected person is one whose tongue and hands never harm others.
4. True nobility is in taqwa — respect people for their character, not their status.

---

## Kindness

### Quran
- **41:34** — *"And not equal are the good deed and the bad. Repel [evil] by that [deed] which is better; and thereupon the one whom between you and him is enmity [will become] as though he was a devoted friend."*
- **16:90** — *"Indeed, Allah orders justice and good conduct (*ihsan*) and giving to relatives."*
- **3:134** — *"Who spend [in the cause of Allah] during ease and hardship and who restrain anger and who pardon the people — and Allah loves the doers of good."*

### Hadith
- *"Allah is kind and loves kindness, and He gives for kindness what He does not give for harshness."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 6927, Sahih Muslim 2593 (**Sahih**)
- *"Kindness is not found in anything without adorning it, and it is not removed from anything without disfiguring it."* — Sahih Muslim 2594 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"Kindness is the outward expression of mercy in the heart. Where mercy is real, kindness flows naturally."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*
- **Ali ibn Abi Talib** — *"Be kind to people as Allah has been kind to you."* — Widely attributed.

### Practical Lessons
1. Kindness adds beauty to every action and its absence corrupts even good deeds.
2. Responding to hostility with kindness can transform enemies into close friends (41:34).
3. Allah gives through kindness what He withholds from those who use harshness.
4. Kindness is not weakness — the Prophet ﷺ was the kindest person who ever lived.

---

## Forgiveness

### Quran
- **3:134** — *"Those who... pardon the people — and Allah loves the doers of good."*
- **42:40** — *"And the retribution for an evil act is one like it, but whoever pardons and makes reconciliation — his reward is [due] from Allah."*
- **24:22** — *"Let them pardon and overlook. Would you not like that Allah should forgive you? And Allah is Forgiving and Merciful."*

### Hadith
- *"Allah does not increase a servant in pardoning others except in honour."* — Sahih Muslim 2588 (**Sahih**)
- *"The strong man is not the one who can wrestle others down. The strong man is the one who controls himself in anger."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 6114, Sahih Muslim 2609 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Umar ibn Al-Khattab** — *"Hold yourself accountable before you are held accountable, and pardon others before you need pardon yourself."* — Widely attributed.
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Forgiving one who wronged you brings a peace to the heart that revenge can never bring."* — *Madarij as-Salikin*

### Practical Lessons
1. Forgiving others does not diminish you — Allah promises it increases your honour (Sahih Muslim 2588).
2. Pardoning and reconciling earns a direct reward from Allah (42:40).
3. Ask yourself: *Would you not like Allah to forgive you?* (24:22) — use this as your motivation to forgive.
4. Forgiveness is for your own peace as much as for the other person.

---

## Honesty

### Quran
- **9:119** — *"O you who have believed, fear Allah and be with the truthful."*
- **33:70–71** — *"O you who have believed, fear Allah and speak words of appropriate justice. He will [then] amend for you your deeds and forgive you your sins."*
- **3:17** — *"[The righteous are] the patient, the true, the obedient, those who spend [in the way of Allah], and those who seek forgiveness before dawn."*

### Hadith
- *"Truthfulness leads to righteousness, and righteousness leads to Paradise... Lying leads to wickedness, and wickedness leads to the Fire."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 6094, Sahih Muslim 2607 (**Sahih**)
- *"It is obligatory upon you to be truthful, for truthfulness leads to goodness, and goodness leads to Paradise."* — Sahih Muslim 2607 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Abu Bakr as-Siddiq** — *"Adhere to truthfulness, for it accompanies all good."* — Widely attributed; Abu Bakr was titled *as-Siddiq* (the Truthful) for his lifelong commitment to honesty.
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Truthfulness is the foundation of all virtues. All character strengths are built on it; when it falls, they all fall."* — *Madarij as-Salikin*

### Practical Lessons
1. Habitual honesty builds a path to Paradise; habitual lying builds a path to the Fire — the Prophet ﷺ stated this directly.
2. Honest speech reforms your deeds and earns forgiveness (33:70–71).
3. Be truthful even when it costs you — that is when truthfulness has its greatest value.
4. Never deceive in business, relationships, or social life — the consequences reach the hereafter.

---

## Trust

### Quran
- **8:27** — *"O you who have believed, do not betray Allah and the Messenger or betray your trusts while you know [what you do]."*
- **4:58** — *"Indeed, Allah commands you to render trusts to whom they are due and when you judge between people to judge with justice."*
- **23:8** — *"And they who are to their trusts and their promises attentive."* (Among the characteristics of successful believers)

### Hadith
- *"There is no faith in one who has no trustworthiness (*amanah*), and there is no religion in one who does not keep his promises."* — Musnad Ahmad 12567 (**Hasan** per Al-Albani)
- *"The signs of a hypocrite are three: when he speaks, he lies; when he promises, he breaks it; when he is entrusted, he betrays."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 33, Sahih Muslim 59 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"Amanah is the foundation of society. When it is lost, no relationship — business, personal, or political — can remain sound."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*
- **Umar ibn Al-Khattab** — *"Hold firm to amanah, for it is the pillar of all dealings between people."* — Widely attributed.

### Practical Lessons
1. Amanah (trustworthiness) is a defining quality of a true believer — its absence is a sign of hypocrisy.
2. Return every trust: a secret shared, money held, a position given — discharge it fully.
3. Render trusts to their rightful owners even if they wronged you — it is Allah's command (4:58).
4. Build a reputation for trustworthiness — it is worth more than any worldly asset.
-e 
---

# Category 3: Personal Development

---

## Responsibility

### Quran
- **2:286** — *"Allah does not burden a soul beyond that it can bear."*
- **74:38** — *"Every soul, for what it has earned, will be retained."*
- **33:72** — *"Indeed, We offered the trust to the heavens and the earth and the mountains, and they declined to bear it... but man [undertook to] bear it. Indeed, he was unjust and ignorant."*

### Hadith
- *"Every one of you is a shepherd and every one of you is responsible for his flock."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 893, Sahih Muslim 1829 (**Sahih**)
- *"On the Day of Judgement, the feet of a servant will not move until he is asked about his life and how he spent it, his knowledge and how he acted on it, his wealth and how he earned and spent it, and his body and how he used it."* — Jami' at-Tirmidhi 2417 (**Hasan**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Umar ibn Al-Khattab** — *"Hold yourself accountable before you are held accountable, and weigh your deeds before they are weighed for you."* — Widely attributed; Ibn al-Qayyim references this in *Madarij as-Salikin*.
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"You will be asked about every moment of your life. Prepare an answer now."* — *Al-Fawa'id*

### Practical Lessons
1. You are a shepherd — of your family, your role, your community. No position is exempt from accountability.
2. On the Day of Judgement, you will be asked specifically about your time, knowledge, wealth, and body.
3. Self-accountability (*muhasabah*) daily is the believer's tool to live responsibly.
4. Accept responsibility for your choices — blaming others is incompatible with Islamic maturity.

---

## Discipline

### Quran
- **103:1–3** — *"By time, indeed mankind is in loss — except for those who have believed and done righteous deeds and advised each other to truth and advised each other to patience."*
- **51:17–18** — *"They used to sleep but little of the night, and in the hours before dawn they would ask forgiveness."*
- **2:45** — *"And seek help through patience and prayer; indeed, it is difficult except for the humbly submissive."*

### Hadith
- *"Take on deeds that you are able to maintain consistently, for Allah does not tire of giving reward until you tire of doing deeds."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 43, Sahih Muslim 783 (**Sahih**)
- *"The most beloved deeds to Allah are the most consistent ones, even if small."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 6464 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Disciplining the soul is harder than disciplining the body. But without it, a person achieves nothing of value in religion or worldly life."* — *Madarij as-Salikin*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"Small consistent acts of worship build the character more than large irregular bursts of devotion."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Consistency over intensity — the Prophet ﷺ confirmed that Allah loves what is maintained regularly, even if small.
2. The companions used to sleep little at night and rise before dawn — discipline in worship creates discipline in life.
3. Build habits anchored to prayer times; the five daily prayers are themselves a discipline training system.
4. Review your daily schedule: does it reflect your stated priorities?

---

## Patience

### Quran
- **2:153** — *"O you who have believed, seek help through patience and prayer. Indeed, Allah is with the patient."*
- **39:10** — *"Indeed, the patient will be given their reward without account."*
- **3:200** — *"O you who have believed, persevere and endure and remain stationed and fear Allah that you may be successful."*

### Hadith
- *"How wonderful is the affair of the believer! If something good happens to him, he is grateful — and that is good for him. If something bad happens to him, he is patient — and that is good for him. And this applies only to the believer."* — Sahih Muslim 2999 (**Sahih**)
- *"Whoever tries to be patient, Allah will give him patience."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 1469, Sahih Muslim 1053 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ali ibn Abi Talib** — *"Patience to faith is like the head to the body. A body without a head is dead; faith without patience is dead."* — Widely attributed.
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Patience is of three kinds: patience in obeying Allah, patience in abstaining from sin, and patience in accepting Allah's decree. Together they are the whole of religion."* — *Uddat as-Sabirin*

### Practical Lessons
1. Patience is rewarded without limit or account (39:10) — no other deed is given this distinction.
2. The believer wins in every situation: gratitude in ease, patience in hardship — both lead to reward.
3. Patience has three forms — in worship, in avoiding sin, and in trials. Strengthen all three.
4. Patience is not passive — it requires active choice, repeated every moment of difficulty.

---

## Gratitude

### Quran
- **14:7** — *"If you are grateful, I will surely increase you [in favour]; but if you deny, indeed My punishment is severe."*
- **2:152** — *"So remember Me; I will remember you. And be grateful to Me and do not deny Me."*
- **31:12** — *"Whoever is grateful is only grateful for [the benefit of] himself. And whoever denies [His favour] — then indeed, Allah is Free of need and Praiseworthy."*

### Hadith
- *"He who does not thank people does not thank Allah."* — Sunan Abu Dawood 4811, Jami' at-Tirmidhi 1954 (**Sahih**)
- *"Allah is pleased with His servant who, when he eats food, praises Him, or when he drinks, praises Him."* — Sahih Muslim 2734 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Gratitude is one of the highest stations of the worshipper. It requires three things: acknowledgment in the heart, praise with the tongue, and using the blessing in obedience to Allah."* — *Madarij as-Salikin*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"The grateful person sees every blessing as a gift that must be acknowledged and protected. The ungrateful sees it as a right and is never satisfied."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Gratitude is guaranteed to increase your blessings — this is Allah's direct promise (14:7).
2. True gratitude has three parts: recognition in the heart, praise on the tongue, and using the blessing correctly.
3. Thanking people is inseparable from thanking Allah — they are linked in the same hadith.
4. Begin and end each day by naming specific blessings. This is the practical foundation of shukr.

---

## Humility

### Quran
- **25:63** — *"And the servants of the Most Merciful are those who walk upon the earth in humility, and when the ignorant address them, they say [words of] peace."*
- **31:18** — *"And do not turn your cheek [in contempt] toward people and do not walk through the earth exultantly. Indeed, Allah does not like everyone self-deluded and boastful."*
- **7:146** — *"I will turn away from My signs those who are arrogant upon the earth without right."*

### Hadith
- *"Charity does not decrease wealth, Allah does not increase a servant in pardoning others except in honour, and no one humbles himself for the sake of Allah except that Allah raises him."* — Sahih Muslim 2588 (**Sahih**)
- *"Allah has revealed to me that you should be humble, so that no one boasts over another and no one oppresses another."* — Sahih Muslim 2865 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"Humility is to see yourself as less than others — and to see any good in you as from Allah alone, not from yourself."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*
- **Hasan al-Basri** — *"The sign of a humble person is that he does not see himself as better than anyone he looks at."* — Widely attributed in classical adab literature.

### Practical Lessons
1. Humility raises you in Allah's sight — arrogance lowers you (Sahih Muslim 2588).
2. The mark of a truly humble person is calm, peaceful responses to the ignorant (25:63).
3. Never walk arrogantly — even your body language is an act of worship or of pride.
4. When praised, return the praise to Allah. Every good in you is from Him.

---

## Self-control

### Quran
- **3:134** — *"Those who... restrain anger and who pardon the people — and Allah loves the doers of good."*
- **53:32** — *"Those who avoid the major sins and immoralities, only [committing] slight ones. Indeed, your Lord is vast in forgiveness."*
- **79:40–41** — *"But as for he who feared the position of his Lord and prevented the soul from [unlawful] inclination — then indeed, Paradise will be [his] refuge."*

### Hadith
- *"The strong man is not the one who can wrestle others down. The strong man is the one who controls himself in anger."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 6114, Sahih Muslim 2609 (**Sahih**)
- *"Do not become angry." He repeated it three times.*— Sahih al-Bukhari 6116 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ali ibn Abi Talib** — *"The greatest victory is a man's victory over himself."* — Widely attributed.
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"The nafs (self) is either your mount to Allah or your mount to destruction. It must be controlled, or it will control you."* — *Madarij as-Salikin*

### Practical Lessons
1. The Islamic definition of strength is self-mastery, not physical dominance.
2. Controlling anger is one of the most repeated practical commands in the Sunnah.
3. Paradise is promised to those who restrain their souls from unlawful desire (79:40–41).
4. Build self-control through fasting — the Prophet ﷺ called it a shield.

---

## Time Management

### Quran
- **103:1–2** — *"By time, indeed mankind is in loss."*
- **23:61** — *"It is those who hasten to good deeds, and they outstrip [others] therein."*
- **63:11** — *"Allah will never delay a soul when its time has come. And Allah is acquainted with what you do."*

### Hadith
- *"Take advantage of five before five: your youth before your old age, your health before your illness, your wealth before your poverty, your free time before your preoccupation, and your life before your death."* — Mustadrak al-Hakim, Al-Bayhaqi (**Sahih** per Al-Albani in *Silsilat al-Ahadith as-Sahihah* 1077)
- *"Two blessings which many people squander: health and free time."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 6412 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Your time is your life. Wasting it is wasting your life. Most people deceive themselves about their time."* — *Al-Fawa'id*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"The hour that passes without benefit is a loss that cannot be recovered. Begin with what matters most."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Allah swore by time (103) — this alone signals its supreme importance.
2. Free time and health are blessings that most people squander — protect both deliberately.
3. Start with what matters most: prayer, then obligations, then what is beneficial.
4. Ask yourself at the end of each day: *What did this day add to my account before Allah?*
-e 
---

# Category 4: Work & Success

---

## Hard Work

### Quran
- **53:39** — *"And that there is not for man except that [good] for which he strives."*
- **9:105** — *"And say: Do [as you will], for Allah will see your deeds, and [so will] His Messenger and the believers."*
- **11:6** — *"And there is no creature on earth but that upon Allah is its provision."*

### Hadith
- *"No one has ever eaten food better than what he earns from the work of his own hands. The Prophet of Allah, Dawud, used to eat from the work of his own hands."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 2072 (**Sahih**)
- *"Allah loves that when any of you does something, he does it with excellence (itqan)."* — Al-Bayhaqi, Shu'ab al-Iman (**Sahih** per Al-Albani)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Umar ibn Al-Khattab** — *"No one should sit back from seeking livelihood and say 'O Allah, provide for me' — for you know that the sky does not rain gold or silver."* — Widely attributed in classical works.
- **Imam Ash-Shafi'i** — *"Work and rely on Allah — for the one who works and relies on Allah is greater than the one who merely waits."* — Widely attributed.

### Practical Lessons
1. Earning through your own hands is praised in Islam — the Prophet Dawud (عليه السلام) is cited as the example.
2. Whatever you do, do it with excellence (itqan) — Allah loves mastery in any work.
3. Work is an act of worship when done with the right intention and by halal means.
4. Do not let tawakkul become passivity — Allah's provision comes through effort, not idleness.

---

## Earning Halal Income

### Quran
- **2:168** — *"O mankind, eat from whatever is on earth [that is] lawful and good and do not follow the footsteps of Satan."*
- **62:10** — *"And when the prayer has been concluded, disperse within the land and seek from the bounty of Allah."*
- **2:188** — *"And do not consume one another's wealth unjustly or send it [in bribery] to the rulers in order that [they might aid] you [to] consume a portion of the wealth of the people in sin, while you know [it is unlawful]."*

### Hadith
- *"The halal is clear and the haram is clear, and between them are doubtful matters. Whoever avoids the doubtful protects his religion and honour."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 52, Sahih Muslim 1599 (**Sahih**)
- *"A time will come upon people when a man will not care whether his wealth comes from halal or haram."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 2059 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Imam Ahmad ibn Hanbal** — *"Seeking halal income is an obligation upon every Muslim."* — Widely attributed in Hanbali fiqh literature.
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Haram in wealth corrupts worship — a du'a made with haram money is veiled from acceptance."* — *Zad al-Ma'ad*

### Practical Lessons
1. Halal income is a religious obligation, not merely a preference.
2. When in doubt, avoid — protecting your deen and honour is worth more than any gain.
3. Haram wealth corrupts your worship and blocks the acceptance of du'a.
4. Regularly audit your income sources for any element of haram or serious doubt.

---

## Excellence (Ihsan)

### Quran
- **16:90** — *"Indeed, Allah orders justice and *ihsan* (excellence/good conduct) and giving to relatives."*
- **55:60** — *"Is the reward for good anything but good?"*
- **2:195** — *"And do good; indeed, Allah loves the doers of good."*

### Hadith
- *"Allah has ordained excellence (ihsan) in all things. So when you kill, do it well; and when you slaughter, do it well. Each of you should sharpen his blade and spare the animal pain."* — Sahih Muslim 1955 (**Sahih**)
- *"Allah loves that when any of you does something, he does it with excellence (itqan)."* — Al-Bayhaqi, Shu'ab al-Iman (**Sahih** per Al-Albani)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"Ihsan is to worship Allah as if you see Him, knowing that even if you do not see Him, He sees you. This awareness transforms every act."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din* (based on the hadith of Jibril)
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Ihsan has two levels: the ihsan of worship — to worship Allah as if you see Him — and the ihsan of service to creation — to give others more than their due."* — *Madarij as-Salikin*

### Practical Lessons
1. Ihsan is commanded by Allah Himself (16:90) alongside justice — it is not optional for a Muslim.
2. Allah loves mastery in work. Doing anything poorly when you can do it well is a failure of Islamic character.
3. Ihsan in worship means feeling Allah's presence — transform your prayer by imagining you stand before Him.
4. Ihsan to people means giving more than you are asked — going beyond the minimum in every relationship.

---

## Leadership

### Quran
- **2:30** — *"And [mention, O Muhammad], when your Lord said to the angels: 'Indeed, I will make upon the earth a successive authority [khalifah].'"*
- **4:58** — *"Indeed, Allah commands you to render trusts to whom they are due and when you judge between people to judge with justice."*
- **32:24** — *"And We made from among them leaders guiding by Our command when they were patient and [when] they were certain of Our signs."*

### Hadith
- *"Each of you is a shepherd and each of you is responsible for his flock."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 893, Sahih Muslim 1829 (**Sahih**)
- *"The most beloved of leaders to Allah is the one who makes [things] easy for people. And the most hated is the one who makes things difficult."* — Sahih Muslim 1828 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ali ibn Abi Talib** — *"Leadership is a trust, not a privilege. Whoever treats it as privilege has betrayed it."* — Widely attributed.
- **Ibn Taymiyyah** — *"Just leadership is among the greatest acts of worship and the most beneficial to the Muslim community."* — *Al-Siyasah al-Shar'iyyah*

### Practical Lessons
1. Every person is a leader — of their family, team, or community. The Prophet ﷺ made no exceptions.
2. The best leaders facilitate, not obstruct. Allah loves the leader who makes things easy.
3. Leadership is a trust (*amanah*) — it will be asked about before Allah.
4. Patience and certainty of faith are the qualities that produce true leadership (32:24).

---

## Justice

### Quran
- **4:135** — *"O you who have believed, be persistently standing firm in justice, witnesses for Allah, even if it be against yourselves or parents and relatives."*
- **5:8** — *"O you who have believed, be persistently standing firm for Allah, witnesses in justice, and do not let the hatred of a people prevent you from being just. Be just; that is nearer to righteousness."*
- **16:90** — *"Indeed, Allah orders justice and good conduct."*

### Hadith
- *"Those who are just will be at minbars of light on the Day of Resurrection, to the right of the Most Merciful — those who are just in their judgements, their families, and what they are in charge of."* — Sahih Muslim 1827 (**Sahih**)
- *"Beware of oppression, for oppression will be darkness on the Day of Resurrection."* — Sahih Muslim 2578 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Umar ibn Al-Khattab** — *"The strongest among you in my sight is the weak person until I have given him his right. And the weakest among you is the strong person until I have taken what is due from him."* — Widely reported; classical sources attribute it to his public address.
- **Ibn Taymiyyah** — *"Allah upholds a just state even if it is disbelieving, and does not uphold an unjust state even if it is Muslim."* — *Al-Siyasah al-Shar'iyyah*

### Practical Lessons
1. Be just even against yourself or your family — the Quran commands it explicitly (4:135).
2. Do not let personal dislike or history prevent you from being fair (5:8).
3. The just will be honoured beside Allah on the Day of Resurrection — this includes parents, managers, judges.
4. Oppression is darkness in the next world — even small injustices carry weight.

---

## Fairness

### Quran
- **4:58** — *"When you judge between people to judge with justice."*
- **6:152** — *"And whenever you speak, be just, even if [it concerns] a near relative."*
- **49:9** — *"And if two factions among the believers should fight, then make settlement between the two. But if one of them oppresses the other, then fight against the one that oppresses until it returns to the ordinance of Allah."*

### Hadith
- *"A man came to the Prophet ﷺ and asked for advice. He said: 'Do not be angry.' He repeated it three times."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 6116 (**Sahih**) *(Anger is cited here as the chief cause of unfairness in human dealings.)*
- *"Whoever has wronged his brother regarding his honour or anything else, let him seek his pardon today before there are no dinars or dirhams."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 2449 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Imam Malik** — *"The foundation of all dealings is that a person gives others what he would wish to receive himself."* — Widely attributed in Maliki fiqh tradition.
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Fairness begins in the heart — the one whose heart is inclined to favour himself over others will never be truly just with people."* — *Madarij as-Salikin*

### Practical Lessons
1. Fairness is commanded even when it goes against a family member (6:152) — truth has no favourites.
2. Settle disputes with justice now, before the Day of Judgement, when no currency will help.
3. Ask yourself in any conflict: *Am I judging the situation or judging the person?*
4. Anger is the greatest enemy of fairness — manage it before making any judgement.

---

## Knowledge

### Quran
- **96:1** — *"Read in the name of your Lord who created."* (The first revealed verse)
- **20:114** — *"And say: My Lord, increase me in knowledge."*
- **58:11** — *"Allah will raise those who have believed among you and those who were given knowledge, by degrees."*

### Hadith
- *"Seeking knowledge is an obligation upon every Muslim."* — Sunan Ibn Majah 224 (**Sahih** per Al-Albani)
- *"Whoever follows a path in pursuit of knowledge, Allah will make easy for him a path to Paradise."* — Sahih Muslim 2699 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ali ibn Abi Talib** — *"Knowledge is better than wealth: knowledge guards you, while you must guard wealth. Knowledge increases with spending; wealth decreases."* — Widely attributed; reported in classical wisdom literature.
- **Imam Ash-Shafi'i** — *"Whoever does not love knowledge does not deserve to be honoured."* — Widely attributed.

### Practical Lessons
1. The first word revealed in the Quran was *Iqra* (Read) — knowledge is the starting point of all Islamic life.
2. Seeking knowledge is an individual obligation (*fard 'ayn*) — it cannot be outsourced.
3. The path of knowledge is the path to Paradise — every step toward learning is a step toward Allah.
4. Teach what you know; knowledge shared does not diminish — it grows.
-e 
---

# Category 5: Mental & Emotional Well-being

---

## Stress

### Quran
- **94:5–6** — *"For indeed, with hardship will be ease. Indeed, with hardship will be ease."*
- **2:286** — *"Allah does not burden a soul beyond that it can bear."*
- **13:28** — *"Verily, in the remembrance of Allah do hearts find rest."*

### Hadith
- *"How wonderful is the affair of the believer! If good befalls him, he is grateful — and that is good for him. If harm befalls him, he is patient — and that is good for him."* — Sahih Muslim 2999 (**Sahih**)
- *"No fatigue, nor disease, nor sorrow, nor sadness, nor hurt, nor distress befalls a Muslim — even the prick of a thorn — but that Allah expiates some of his sins."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 5641, Sahih Muslim 2573 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"The heart has no peace except through its Lord. Seeking peace through anything else only increases the pain."* — *Al-Fawa'id*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"Most stress comes from fear of what has not yet happened. Return to the present moment and to trust in Allah — that is where relief is found."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din* (paraphrase)

### Practical Lessons
1. Ease follows every hardship — Allah repeats this twice in Surah 94 for emphasis.
2. No burden is placed on you that you cannot bear (2:286) — this is a divine guarantee.
3. Dhikr (remembrance of Allah) is the Quranic prescription for a restless heart (13:28).
4. Reframe stress: even the prick of a thorn expiates sins. Nothing is wasted before Allah.

---

## Anxiety

### Quran
- **10:62** — *"Verily, the friends of Allah — no fear will there be concerning them, nor will they grieve."*
- **2:38** — *"Whoever follows My guidance — no fear will there be concerning them, nor will they grieve."*
- **3:173** — *"And they said: 'Sufficient for us is Allah, and [He is] the best Disposer of affairs.' So they returned with favour from Allah and bounty, no harm having touched them."*

### Hadith
- *"Ask Allah for wellbeing (*al-'afiyah*), for no one is given anything better than wellbeing."* — Jami' at-Tirmidhi 3512 (**Sahih** per Al-Albani)
- *"O Allah, I seek refuge in You from worry and grief, from incapacity and laziness, from cowardice and miserliness, and from the burden of debt and from being overpowered by men."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 6363 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Anxiety about the future is one of the greatest weapons of Shaytan against the believer. Most of what people fear never comes to pass."* — *Al-Fawa'id*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"Trust in Allah's wisdom transforms anxiety into anticipation. When you know He is managing your affairs, you can rest."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din* (paraphrase)

### Practical Lessons
1. The friends of Allah are free from fear and grief (10:62) — taqwa is the path to inner peace.
2. Say *hasbunallah wa ni'mal wakil* (3:173) in moments of anxiety — it is a prophetically proven formula.
3. Make the du'a for relief from worry a daily supplication; the Prophet ﷺ taught it explicitly (Bukhari 6363).
4. Anxiety about the future is often a failure of tawakkul — practice surrendering the outcome to Allah.

---

## Hope

### Quran
- **39:53** — *"Say: O My servants who have transgressed against themselves [by sinning], do not despair of the mercy of Allah. Indeed, Allah forgives all sins. Indeed, it is He who is the Forgiving, the Merciful."*
- **15:56** — *"[Ibrahim] said: 'And who despairs of the mercy of his Lord except for those astray?'"*
- **12:87** — *"Do not despair of relief from Allah. Indeed, no one despairs of relief from Allah except the disbelieving people."*

### Hadith
- *"Allah said: 'I am as My servant expects Me to be. I am with him when he mentions Me.'"* — Sahih al-Bukhari 7405, Sahih Muslim 2675 (**Sahih**)
- *"None of you should die except while he has good expectations of Allah."* — Sahih Muslim 2877 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Hope is the fuel of the heart's journey to Allah. Without it, the traveller stops walking."* — *Madarij as-Salikin*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"Hope is not mere wishing. It is the state of a heart that knows Allah's mercy is real and acts accordingly."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Despair of Allah's mercy is explicitly condemned — even the greatest sinner should never lose hope (39:53).
2. Allah treats you according to your expectations of Him — think well of Allah always.
3. Maintain good expectations of Allah especially at the time of death (Sahih Muslim 2877).
4. Hope paired with action is the Islamic balance — not passive wishful thinking, but expectant striving.

---

## Sadness

### Quran
- **2:155–157** — *"And We will surely test you with something of fear and hunger... but give good tidings to the patient — who, when disaster strikes them, say: 'Indeed we belong to Allah, and indeed to Him we will return.' Those are the ones upon whom are blessings from their Lord and mercy."*
- **12:86** — *"[Yaqub] said: 'I only complain of my suffering and my grief to Allah.'"*
- **94:5** — *"For indeed, with hardship will be ease."*

### Hadith
- *"No fatigue, nor disease, nor sorrow, nor sadness, nor hurt, nor distress befalls a Muslim — even the prick of a thorn — but that Allah expiates some of his sins."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 5641 (**Sahih**)
- *"Recite the du'a: Allahumma inni 'abduka... [O Allah, I am Your servant...] — whoever says it with certainty in his heart, Allah will drive away his sorrow and replace his grief with joy."* — Sunan Abu Dawood 1555 (**Sahih** per Al-Albani)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Sadness, when accepted with patience and directed toward Allah, purifies the heart as fire purifies gold."* — *Madarij as-Salikin*
- **Hasan al-Basri** — *"The believer wakes up sad and goes to sleep sad — and this is fitting, for he is between two great matters: what has passed of his sins, and what remains of his life."* — Widely attributed.

### Practical Lessons
1. Take your grief to Allah directly, as Prophet Yaqub did — not to social media or mere venting.
2. Sadness is not a sign of weak faith — the prophets experienced deep grief.
3. Use the prophetic du'a for sorrow (Abu Dawood 1555) — it is a specific prescription, not general advice.
4. Know that every pain expiates sins — nothing a believer suffers is meaningless before Allah.

---

## Anger

### Quran
- **3:134** — *"Those who... restrain anger and who pardon the people — and Allah loves the doers of good."*
- **41:34** — *"Repel [evil] by that [deed] which is better... thereupon the one whom between you and him is enmity will become as though he was a devoted friend."*
- **42:37** — *"And those who avoid the major sins and immoralities, and when they are angry, they forgive."*

### Hadith
- *"Do not become angry." [The Prophet ﷺ was asked for advice; he repeated this three times.]* — Sahih al-Bukhari 6116 (**Sahih**)
- *"When one of you becomes angry while standing, let him sit down. If the anger leaves him, well and good; otherwise let him lie down."* — Sunan Abu Dawood 4782 (**Sahih** per Al-Albani)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ali ibn Abi Talib** — *"The first to apologise is the bravest. The first to forgive is the strongest."* — Widely attributed.
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Anger is an ember in the heart. When it is stoked, it burns the one who holds it before it burns anyone else."* — *Madarij as-Salikin*

### Practical Lessons
1. The Prophet ﷺ's most repeated practical advice was a single command: *"Do not become angry."*
2. Physical remedies work: sit when standing, lie down when sitting, perform wudu.
3. Restraining anger in the moment is one of the explicit qualities of those whom Allah loves (3:134).
4. Forgiving when angry is the mark of those who avoid major sins (42:37).

---

## Fear

### Quran
- **2:38** — *"Whoever follows My guidance — no fear will there be concerning them, nor will they grieve."*
- **3:175** — *"It is only Satan who frightens [you] of his supporters. So fear them not, but fear Me, if you are [truly] believers."*
- **9:40** — *"Do not grieve; indeed Allah is with us."* (The Prophet ﷺ to Abu Bakr in the cave)

### Hadith
- *"Seek help against fear by saying: La ilaha illa Allah."* — Not a single verified hadith; rather scholars derive this from general principles of dhikr in texts such as Sahih al-Bukhari's *Kitab al-Du'a*.
- *"Whoever says upon leaving his house: 'Bismillah, tawakkaltu 'ala Allah, wa la hawla wa la quwwata illa billah' — it is said to him: You are guided, protected, and guarded, and Shaytan moves away from him."* — Sunan Abu Dawood 5095, Jami' at-Tirmidhi 3426 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Fear of other than Allah arises from distance from Allah. Draw near to Allah and the fear of creation disappears."* — *Al-Fawa'id*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"Khashyah (awe-fear of Allah) is the most beneficial fear; khawf (fear of harm) is beneficial only when it drives you toward preparation and reliance on Allah."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Fear of creation dissolves in proportion to your closeness to Allah — draw near to Him to find courage.
2. Satan's method is to magnify human threats. The Quran's answer is direct: *"Fear Me, not them"* (3:175).
3. Recite the morning du'a before leaving home (Abu Dawood 5095) — it is a prophetic shield.
4. The only fear worth cultivating is khashyah of Allah — it produces wisdom and protects from every other fear.

---

## Depression

### Quran
- **93:3–5** — *"Your Lord has not taken leave of you, [O Muhammad], nor has He detested [you]. And the Hereafter is better for you than the first [life]. And your Lord is going to give you, and you will be satisfied."*
- **94:5–6** — *"For indeed, with hardship will be ease. Indeed, with hardship will be ease."*
- **39:53** — *"Do not despair of the mercy of Allah."*

### Hadith
- *"No fatigue, nor disease, nor sorrow... befalls a Muslim... but that Allah expiates some of his sins."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 5641 (**Sahih**)
- The Prophet ﷺ himself experienced periods of intense distress — the *fatra* (pause in revelation) caused him great anguish, after which Surah 93 was revealed as direct divine comfort.

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Sadness of the heart, when not accompanied by sin, is itself a form of worship — for it shows the heart is alive and knows it is far from its Lord."* — *Madarij as-Salikin*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"The greatest cause of spiritual depression is isolation from Allah. The cure is return — even if the return is imperfect, even if the steps are small."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din* (paraphrase)

### Practical Lessons
1. Allah addressed the Prophet ﷺ directly in his darkest period (Surah 93) — no believer is ever abandoned by Allah.
2. Do not despair of Allah's mercy even at the lowest points — this itself is the Quranic command (39:53).
3. Seek professional help without shame — seeking a doctor for mental health is equivalent to seeking one for a physical illness. Islam recognises the reality of illness in all its forms.
4. Small steps matter: wudu, a short prayer, one ayah of Quran — begin where you are.

---

## Motivation

### Quran
- **3:139** — *"Do not weaken and do not grieve, and you will be superior if you are [true] believers."*
- **13:11** — *"Indeed, Allah will not change the condition of a people until they change what is in themselves."*
- **94:7** — *"So when you have finished [your duties], then stand up [for worship]."*

### Hadith
- *"Make use of five before five: your youth before your old age, your health before your illness, your wealth before your poverty, your free time before your preoccupation, and your life before your death."* — Al-Bayhaqi (**Sahih** per Al-Albani, *Silsilat al-Ahadith as-Sahihah* 1077)
- *"Be eager for what benefits you, seek help from Allah, and do not lose heart."* — Sahih Muslim 2664 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Aspiration is the engine of the heart. Whoever has great aspirations for the akhirah will find energy for this world; whoever loses his akhirah aspiration loses energy for everything."* — *Al-Fawa'id*
- **Ali ibn Abi Talib** — *"Whoever knows himself, knows his Lord. And whoever knows his Lord, works for Him."* — Widely attributed; foundational in Sufi and classical Islamic thought.

### Practical Lessons
1. Change begins from within (13:11) — Allah does not change your situation until you change your interior state.
2. Your life is a finite window (the hadith of five before five) — use this as your daily motivating reality.
3. Be eager, not passive — *"be eager for what benefits you"* is a direct prophetic command.
4. Connect your motivation to the akhirah: short-term goals exhaust; eternal goals sustain.
-e 
---

# Category 6: Society

---

## Helping Others

### Quran
- **2:177** — *"Righteousness is... to give wealth, in spite of love for it, to relatives, orphans, the needy, the traveller, those who ask [for help]."*
- **107:1–3** — *"Have you seen the one who denies the [Day of] Recompense? For that is the one who drives away the orphan and does not encourage the feeding of the poor."*
- **5:2** — *"And cooperate in righteousness and piety, but do not cooperate in sin and aggression."*

### Hadith
- *"Whoever relieves a Muslim of a worldly hardship, Allah will relieve him of a hardship on the Day of Resurrection. Whoever makes things easy for a person in difficulty, Allah will make things easy for him in this world and the next."* — Sahih Muslim 2699 (**Sahih**)
- *"The best of people are those who are most beneficial to people."* — Al-Mu'jam al-Awsat, Al-Tabarani (**Hasan** per Al-Albani)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"Service to the creation is service to the Creator. The one who helps the weak and the needy is in Allah's service."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*
- **Hasan al-Basri** — *"If you want to know your worth before Allah, look at where Allah has placed you in the service of His creation."* — Widely attributed.

### Practical Lessons
1. Relieving someone's hardship is directly rewarded with relief on the Day of Judgement.
2. The best human being is the one most beneficial to people — service to others is the measure of greatness.
3. Denying help to orphans and the poor is linked in the Quran to denying the Day of Judgement itself (107).
4. Cooperate in good actively — waiting to be asked is not enough.

---

## Charity

### Quran
- **2:261** — *"The example of those who spend their wealth in the way of Allah is like a seed [of grain] which grows seven spikes; in each spike is a hundred grains."*
- **63:10** — *"And spend from what We have provided you before death approaches one of you."*
- **3:92** — *"Never will you attain the good [reward] until you spend [in the way of Allah] from that which you love."*

### Hadith
- *"Charity does not decrease wealth. Allah does not increase a servant in pardoning others except in honour, and no one humbles himself for Allah except that Allah raises him."* — Sahih Muslim 2588 (**Sahih**)
- *"Protect yourself from the Fire, even with half a date."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 1413, Sahih Muslim 1016 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Charity extinguishes sins as water extinguishes fire. It heals the body, purifies the wealth, and opens doors that no other key can open."* — *Al-Wabil as-Sayyib*
- **Ali ibn Abi Talib** — *"Give in charity before your wealth is taken from you."* — Widely attributed.

### Practical Lessons
1. Charity multiplies — a single act of giving is counted as seven hundred or more before Allah (2:261).
2. No amount is too small — even half a date can protect from the Fire.
3. Give from what you love, not merely from what you can spare (3:92) — that is the level of true sadaqah.
4. Establish consistent charity (sadaqah jariyah) that continues after your death: a well, a school, a shared Quran.

---

## Community

### Quran
- **49:10** — *"The believers are but brothers, so make settlement between your brothers. And fear Allah that you may receive mercy."*
- **3:103** — *"And hold firmly to the rope of Allah all together and do not become divided."*
- **9:71** — *"The believing men and believing women are allies of one another. They enjoin what is right and forbid what is wrong."*

### Hadith
- *"The believers in their mutual love, mercy, and compassion are like one body — when one part suffers, the rest of the body responds with sleeplessness and fever."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 6011, Sahih Muslim 2586 (**Sahih**)
- *"Jama'ah (the community) is a mercy and division is a punishment."* — Musnad Ahmad (**Hasan** per scholars of hadith)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn Taymiyyah** — *"The Muslim community is a single entity. Its strength is the strength of all; its weakness is the weakness of all."* — *Majmu' al-Fatawa*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"A Muslim is obligated to contribute to his community — through his time, his wealth, his knowledge, or at minimum his prayer."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Muslims are one body — what affects your neighbour or community affects you.
2. Division is a punishment; unity is a mercy — work actively to resolve disputes and maintain community bonds.
3. Enjoining good and forbidding wrong is a shared communal duty, not only for scholars or leaders.
4. Contribute something to your community — time, wealth, knowledge, or prayer for it.

---

## Respect for People

### Quran
- **17:70** — *"And We have certainly honoured the children of Adam."*
- **49:11** — *"Do not let a people ridicule another people... do not insult one another and do not call each other by [offensive] nicknames."*
- **49:12** — *"Avoid much [negative] assumption... do not spy, and do not backbite one another."*

### Hadith
- *"Your blood, your wealth, and your honour are sacred among you, as sacred as this day of yours, in this month of yours, in this city of yours."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 1741, Sahih Muslim 1679 (**Sahih**, Farewell Sermon)
- *"The Muslim is the one from whose tongue and hand the people are safe."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 10 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Umar ibn Al-Khattab** — *"Hold people in good esteem until you know otherwise."* — Widely attributed.
- **Imam An-Nawawi** — *"Among the most important rights of a Muslim upon another is the protection of his honour — in his presence and his absence."* — *Riyadh as-Salihin*

### Practical Lessons
1. Every human being has been honoured by Allah (17:70) — disrespect contradicts the Quran's foundational view of humanity.
2. The Prophet ﷺ made honour as sacred as blood and wealth in his final public address.
3. Protect people's honour in their absence — backbiting and slander are major sins.
4. Assume the best of people until you know otherwise; suspicion is itself forbidden (49:12).

---

## Keeping Promises

### Quran
- **5:1** — *"O you who have believed, fulfil [all] contracts."*
- **17:34** — *"And fulfil [every] commitment. Indeed, the commitment is ever [that about which one will be] questioned."*
- **9:4** — *"So fulfil for them their treaty to the end of its term."*

### Hadith
- *"The signs of a hypocrite are three: when he speaks, he lies; when he promises, he breaks it; when he is entrusted, he betrays."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 33, Sahih Muslim 59 (**Sahih**)
- *"There is no faith in one who has no trustworthiness, and there is no religion in one who does not keep his promises."* — Musnad Ahmad 12567 (**Hasan** per Al-Albani)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ali ibn Abi Talib** — *"Do not promise what you cannot fulfil, for the tongue is the messenger of the heart — and a lying tongue exposes a betraying heart."* — Widely attributed.
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"Breaking a promise is a form of lying — and it corrupts trust in the relationship that no amount of good deeds can easily repair."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Breaking promises is a defining sign of hypocrisy — the bar is extremely high in Islam.
2. Every commitment will be questioned on the Day of Judgement (17:34).
3. Do not make a promise unless you intend to keep it — better to say *"insha'Allah I will try"* than to commit carelessly.
4. If you cannot fulfil a commitment, communicate early and honestly.

---

## Good Manners

### Quran
- **25:63** — *"The servants of the Most Merciful are those who walk upon the earth in humility, and when the ignorant address them, they say [words of] peace."*
- **31:19** — *"And be moderate in your pace and lower your voice; indeed, the most disagreeable of sounds is the voice of donkeys."*
- **3:159** — *"So by mercy from Allah, [O Muhammad], you were lenient with them. And if you had been rude in speech and harsh in heart, they would have disbanded from about you."*

### Hadith
- *"The heaviest thing placed on the scales on the Day of Resurrection will be good character."* — Jami' at-Tirmidhi 2002 (**Sahih** per Al-Albani)
- *"I was sent only to perfect good character."* — Al-Muwatta, Musnad Ahmad (**Sahih** per Al-Albani)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"Good manners are the fruit of good character. A person with a well-trained soul naturally speaks well, walks with dignity, and treats people with care."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"The Prophet ﷺ was sent to perfect character — meaning that character is the core of the religion, and all ritual worship exists to polish it."* — *Zad al-Ma'ad*

### Practical Lessons
1. The Prophet ﷺ described his entire mission as perfecting good character — it is not secondary to worship.
2. Good character is the heaviest thing on the scales of judgement — prioritise it above status and appearance.
3. Gentleness and lenience in speech draws people toward Islam; harshness drives them away (3:159).
4. Good manners begin with self: moderate your voice, your walk, your look, your words.
-e 
---

# Category 7: Health

---

## Sports

### Quran
- **8:60** — *"And prepare against them whatever you are able of power and of steeds of war by which you may terrify the enemy of Allah and your enemy."*
- **2:195** — *"And do not throw [yourselves] with your [own] hands into destruction."*

### Hadith
- *"The strong believer is better and more beloved to Allah than the weak believer, though both are good."* — Sahih Muslim 2664 (**Sahih**)
- *"Teach your children swimming, archery, and horse-riding."* — Al-Bayhaqi, Shu'ab al-Iman (**Hasan** per scholars; widely cited by Imam An-Nawawi)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Imam An-Nawawi** — *"Physical training and activities that strengthen the body for worship and service are encouraged in Islam."* — *Sharh Sahih Muslim*
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"The body is a trust — neglecting its strength is a form of ingratitude for the gift of health."* — *Zad al-Ma'ad*

### Practical Lessons
1. Physical strength is beloved to Allah — a strong believer is better than a weak one in both worship and service.
2. Exercise and physical training are acts of preserving a trust — your body belongs to Allah.
3. Sport that builds discipline, health, and community cohesion is encouraged in the Sunnah.
4. Choose activities that strengthen the body without harming it through excess or forbidden means.

---

## Physical Strength

### Quran
- **8:60** — *"And prepare against them whatever you are able of power."*
- **28:26** — *"One of them said: 'O my father, hire him. Indeed, the best one you can hire is the strong and the trustworthy.'"*
- **2:247** — *"Allah has chosen him [the king] over you and has increased him in knowledge and stature."* (Physical stature listed as a divine gift of leadership)

### Hadith
- *"The strong believer is better and more beloved to Allah than the weak believer, though both are good."* — Sahih Muslim 2664 (**Sahih**)
- *"Your body has a right over you."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 1975 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Physical health is the foundation of all other forms of health — spiritual, mental, and social. Its preservation is one of the five objectives of Islamic law (*maqasid*)."* — *Zad al-Ma'ad*
- **Umar ibn Al-Khattab** — *"Teach your children swimming, archery, and riding. A man who cannot defend himself and his family has fallen short."* — Widely attributed; related to the narration in Al-Bayhaqi.

### Practical Lessons
1. Strength is an Islamic value — physical capability enables worship, family protection, and community service.
2. Your body has rights over you (Sahih al-Bukhari 1975) — rest, exercise, and care are religious obligations.
3. Preserving physical health is one of the five objectives (*maqasid*) of Islamic law.
4. Build strength with the intention of better worship and service — this transforms exercise into an act of worship.

---

## Cleanliness

### Quran
- **2:222** — *"Indeed, Allah loves those who are constantly repentant and loves those who purify themselves."*
- **74:4–5** — *"And your clothing purify. And uncleanliness avoid."*
- **9:108** — *"Within it are men who love to purify themselves; and Allah loves those who purify themselves."*

### Hadith
- *"Purity is half of faith."* — Sahih Muslim 223 (**Sahih**)
- *"The fitra consists of five things: circumcision, shaving the pubic hair, trimming the moustache, cutting the nails, and plucking the underarm hair."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 5889, Sahih Muslim 257 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Imam An-Nawawi** — *"Purity (*taharah*) is the key to prayer and the foundation of a Muslim's daily life. Without it, the most essential act of worship is invalid."* — *Al-Majmu'*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"External cleanliness reflects internal cleanliness. The one who maintains his body and environment pure is better prepared for the purity of the heart."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Purity is half of faith — it is not a minor detail but a foundational pillar of Islamic life.
2. Wudu (ablution) is required five times a day — it keeps a Muslim in a near-constant state of physical and spiritual cleanliness.
3. The fitra practices (personal hygiene standards) are Sunnah — maintain them regularly.
4. Clean environment, clean clothing, and clean body all contribute to the quality of worship and mental clarity.

---

## Healthy Lifestyle

### Quran
- **7:31** — *"O children of Adam, take your adornment at every masjid, and eat and drink, but be not excessive. Indeed, He likes not those who commit excess."*
- **2:195** — *"And do not throw [yourselves] with your [own] hands into destruction."*
- **5:4** — *"They ask you what has been made lawful for them. Say: Lawful for you are [all] good things."*

### Hadith
- *"The son of Adam does not fill any vessel worse than his stomach. A few morsels are enough to keep his back straight. But if he must, then a third for food, a third for drink, and a third for his breath."* — Jami' at-Tirmidhi 2380, Sunan Ibn Majah 3349 (**Hasan** per At-Tirmidhi)
- *"Your body has a right over you."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 1975 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"More than half of medicine is in moderation of food and drink. Most illness enters the body through the stomach."* — *Zad al-Ma'ad*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"The healthy body is the vehicle of the soul. Neglecting it weakens worship; abusing it destroys it."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Moderation is the Islamic standard for everything — excess in food is explicitly condemned (7:31).
2. Eat to a third, drink to a third, leave a third for air — this prophetic guide is medically validated.
3. Avoid what harms the body (2:195) — smoking, intoxicants, and harmful substances are covered under this verse.
4. A healthy body enables better prayer, more energy for family, and longer life in service of Allah.

---

## Sleep

### Quran
- **30:23** — *"And of His signs is your sleep by night and day and your seeking of His bounty. Indeed in that are signs for a people who listen."*
- **25:47** — *"And it is He who has made the night for you as clothing and sleep [a means of] rest and has made the day a resurrection."*
- **78:9** — *"And made your sleep [a means for] rest."*

### Hadith
- *"When any of you goes to bed, let him dust off his bed with the inside of his lower garment, for he does not know what is on it."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 6320 (**Sahih**)
- *"The Prophet ﷺ disliked sleeping before 'Isha prayer and talking after it."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 568 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"The Sunnah of sleep — on the right side, with dhikr, after Witr — makes sleep itself a form of worship."* — *Zad al-Ma'ad*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"Sleep is not the enemy of worship — improper sleep is. The one who sleeps in the Sunnah manner and rises for Fajr has made his night wholly productive."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Sleep is a sign of Allah (30:23) — treat it as a divine gift to be used wisely.
2. Sleep before midnight and rise for Fajr — the Sunnah timetable aligns with natural biological rhythms.
3. Follow the bedtime Sunnah: sleep on your right side, read *Ayat al-Kursi*, recite the three Quls — it turns sleep into an act of worship.
4. Avoid excessive sleep; the Prophet ﷺ disliked sleeping before 'Isha except for necessity.

---

## Eating

### Quran
- **2:168** — *"O mankind, eat from whatever is on earth [that is] lawful and good."*
- **7:31** — *"Eat and drink, but be not excessive. Indeed, He likes not those who commit excess."*
- **80:24** — *"Then let mankind look at his food."*

### Hadith
- *"A third for food, a third for drink, and a third for his breath."* — Jami' at-Tirmidhi 2380 (**Hasan**)
- *"Begin your meal with bismillah, eat with your right hand, and eat from what is nearest to you."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 5376, Sahih Muslim 2022 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Eating halal food in moderation is a condition for the acceptance of du'a and the soundness of worship."* — *Zad al-Ma'ad*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"Hunger sharpens the intellect, softens the heart, and weakens desires. Overeating does the opposite to all three."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Say *bismillah* before eating and eat with the right hand — this is a simple Sunnah with great reward.
2. Eat from what is nearest to you and do not overreach — even table manners reflect Islamic ethics.
3. Leave one third of your stomach empty — this is the prophetic standard and is medically sound.
4. Eating halal and tayyib (good) food is a condition for spiritual clarity; haram food clouds the heart.
-e 
---

# Category 8: Spirituality

---

## Prayer

### Quran
- **2:45** — *"And seek help through patience and prayer; indeed, it is difficult except for the humbly submissive."*
- **29:45** — *"Recite what has been revealed to you of the Book and establish prayer. Indeed, prayer prohibits immorality and wrongdoing."*
- **20:14** — *"Indeed, I am Allah. There is no deity except Me, so worship Me and establish prayer for My remembrance."*

### Hadith
- *"The first matter that the servant will be brought to account for on the Day of Judgement is prayer. If it is sound, all his deeds will be sound. If it is corrupt, all his deeds will be corrupt."* — Al-Mu'jam al-Awsat, At-Tabarani (**Hasan** per Al-Albani)
- *"Between a man and disbelief and polytheism is the abandonment of prayer."* — Sahih Muslim 82 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Prayer is the pillar of the religion. It is the nourishment of the soul, the light of the heart, and the link between the servant and his Lord."* — *Kitab as-Salah*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"The outward form of prayer is movement and words. Its reality is the presence of the heart before Allah. Without the heart, the prayer is a shell."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Prayer is the first deed judged on the Day of Judgement — if it is sound, everything else follows.
2. Prayer actively prevents immorality (29:45) — it is not only a duty but a shield.
3. Pray on time without delay — the Prophet ﷺ named it the most beloved deed to Allah (Sahih al-Bukhari 527).
4. Focus on presence of heart (*khushu'*) — the prayer of a distracted heart is valid but loses its transformative power.

---

## Tawakkul

### Quran
- **65:3** — *"And whoever relies upon Allah — then He is sufficient for him. Indeed, Allah will accomplish His purpose."*
- **3:159** — *"And when you have decided, then rely upon Allah. Indeed, Allah loves those who rely [upon Him]."*
- **14:12** — *"And why should we not rely upon Allah while He has guided us to our [good] ways."*

### Hadith
- *"If you were to rely upon Allah with true reliance, He would provide for you as He provides for the birds — they go out in the morning empty and return in the evening full."* — Jami' at-Tirmidhi 2344 (**Hasan** per At-Tirmidhi)
- *"Tie your camel, then put your trust in Allah."* — Jami' at-Tirmidhi 2517 (**Hasan**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Ibn al-Qayyim** — *"Tawakkul is not abandoning effort — it is the heart's certainty that the outcome belongs to Allah alone while the limbs do their part."* — *Madarij as-Salikin*
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"The sign of true tawakkul is the tranquillity of the heart in all circumstances — not the absence of effort, but the absence of anxiety about the result."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*

### Practical Lessons
1. Tawakkul requires action first — tie your camel, then trust Allah. Passivity is not tawakkul.
2. True reliance on Allah is rewarded with provision the way birds are provided for — going out empty and returning full.
3. Tawakkul eliminates anxiety about outcomes — you do your part and surrender the rest.
4. After making a decision, commit to tawakkul fully (3:159) — second-guessing collapses trust.

---

## Sincerity

### Quran
- **98:5** — *"And they were not commanded except to worship Allah, [being] sincere to Him in religion, inclining to truth."*
- **39:11** — *"Say: Indeed, I have been commanded to worship Allah, [being] sincere to Him in religion."*
- **18:110** — *"So whoever would hope for the meeting with his Lord — let him do righteous work and not associate in the worship of his Lord anyone."*

### Hadith
- *"Actions are [judged] by intentions, and every person will have what he intended."* — Sahih al-Bukhari 1, Sahih Muslim 1907 (**Sahih**)
- *"Allah does not look at your appearance or your wealth, but He looks at your hearts and your deeds."* — Sahih Muslim 2564 (**Sahih**)

### Scholar Quotes
- **Imam Al-Ghazali** — *"Sincerity is to direct every act solely toward Allah. If even a trace of desire for praise, status, or worldly gain enters, the act is corrupted."* — *Ihya' Ulum ad-Din*
